QUESTION: If there is an outstanding loan at the time the second insured dies, how does it affect the death benefit paid?
CATEGORY: CAT4_MULTIHOP_REASONING

====================================================================================================

rewritten_query: How does an outstanding loan affect the death benefit in a second-to-die life insurance policy?
reasoning: This query is asking about the financial implications of an outstanding loan on a life insurance policy at the time of the second insured's death. The key elements are: "outstanding loan," "second insured," and "death benefit paid." This is a policy-specific question that depends on the terms of the insurance contract, particularly how loans are handled in a joint life policy (common in second-to-die policies). The answer hinges on whether the loan reduces the death benefit, and whether the policy has a clause about loan offsets. Since the query does not mention a specific policy or insurer, it cannot be answered with a single lookup. However, the core logic is: first, confirm the policy type (likely a second-to-die policy), then determine how outstanding loans are treated in such policies. The answer is not a single fact but a chain: the existence of a loan affects the death benefit only if the policy terms specify that loans reduce the payout. Therefore, this is a two-step reasoning process: first, identify the policy type (second-to-die), then determine the loan offset rule.
